# The AAS volumes of 1909–1925 (AAS 1–17): the phase-2b-iii-b report

Generated by `npx tsx tools/acta-volumes-report.ts 1909-1925` from `data/documents/*.json` and `tools/fixtures/acta/` on 2026-09-21 —
the report of phase 2b-iii-b of [#25](https://github.com/CatholicOS/cmddr/issues/25) as the
[acta volumes spec](../specs/2026-09-13-acta-volumes-design.md) §6 and §10 define it: the seventeen whole-volume OCR PDFs
of 1909–1925 -- Pius X to his death (20 August 1914), Benedict XV (AAS 6 carries both) to his death (22 January 1922), and
Pius XI's first three years (AAS 14 carries both) -- whose text layer lost the page column on most index pages (spec §10.1),
their chronological-index pages extracted by `tools/fetch-acta.sh` in pypdf's layout mode from the PDFs kept in the local
store (`tools/fixtures/acta/README.md` records page ranges, modes and retrieval), and the pages the OCR lost read back from
the volume bodies into the sidecars beside the fixtures (`tools/recover-acta-pages.ts`, spec §10.3; §1b). Each is parsed
(`tools/src/acta/index.ts`, generalised across the century's typography and OCR), classified (`categories.ts`), its pope
headings mapped (`popes.ts`), and matched to the shelf records of Pius X → Benedict XVI by issuer, date and incipit
(`match.ts`); what did not match, in a category the registry creates from the *Acta* for that pope, became a document
(`create.ts`, the phase-2a rule and guard unchanged), and everything else is listed here with its reason. The join and the
creator run over every source at once, as the harvest does; the ten index PDFs of 2015–2024 keep their own report
(`2026-09-12-acta-join-2015-2024.md`, regenerated). The *belief* beside an unmatched entry is the script's heuristic; the
reading in the prose is the author's.

## 1. Parse rate per source

Entries parsed over the lines of the pope parts that end in a page number (spec §4), with the lines consumed without an
entry; the same over the harvested categories alone (what the join acts on); *opened without a page* counts the entries a
date line opened that never reached a page number, *month-only* the entries the index dates to the month (a printed
month, a blank day: kept, matched by incipit within the month, never created). A source under 95 % is explained below,
not used silently.

### The reading

1. **The volume bodies give back 770 of the 1136 pages the index OCR lost (67.8 %), and no volume clears the floor.** Of the
   1480 entries the seventeen indexes open, 1136 close without a page (spec §10.1); the recovery (§1b) reads 770 back from
   the body, by the only hit inside the category's runs of the *Index generalis*
   (732), by the only hit within one OCR character (25) or by the dating formula that settles a tie (13), and
   17 more are read by hand (`ACTA_PAGE_READINGS`, §2.1). 349 stay without one, and
   the rate after recovery -- entries with a page over every line that ended in a page or opened without one, 1131 / 1575 = 71.8 % over
   the era, 78.4 % without AAS 1 -- is under 95 % in every source; the highest are 1917-I (91.2 %), 1912 (90.9 %), 1911 (90.4 %). Three
   things keep it there, each measured. (a) An index that describes an act without its incipit gives the recovery nothing to
   search for: 205 of the 349 (§1b, *no incipit*) -- 108 in AAS 1 (1909), whose index prints incipits only in guillemets after a
   genre word (the sample report §1.2; 111 of its 113 entries open without a page and 3 come back, *Promulgandi* at p. 5 and
   *Communium rerum* at p. 333), and the rest the consistories' items (*Sacri Pallii expostulatio*, *Tituli assignati*), the
   canonisation lists of 1925 and the letters whose index line the OCR broke before the incipit. (b) An incipit that opens no
   paragraph of the body, or opens several with no formula to tell them apart: 61 *none* and 58 *several*, 119 letters and
   apostolic letters whose body the OCR damaged at the opening (*Sapienti Consilio* at AAS 1 p. 7 prints a drop capital the
   text layer reads `S` / `apienti`) or whose incipit -- *Quae catholico nomini*, *Ex hac*, *Dilectus filius* -- opens two or
   three acts of the volume; 26 open a page outside the category's runs, 14 a page whose running header contradicts its
   number, and 2 (*claimants*) a page another entry of the same category and incipit was given -- the two letters *Communis vestra*
   the 1915 index dates 10 November, whose one formula of that date is p. 569's (the Ligurian bishops'; the Brazilian
   bishops' opens at p. 591, dated `die ix decembris MCMXV`, the index's `Dec.` and `10` set by the layout mode on lines below its
   incipit), so neither is given the page. (c) Lines that end in a page and are not acts: 95 across the era, the consistory items of
   AAS 10 (1918, 21 of them), AAS 14 (1922, 13) and AAS 1 (1909, 25), which the parse rate has always counted (spec §4) and the
   rate after recovery inherits. The *Harvested, after* column, which leaves the consistories out, puts 1918 at 87.7 % where
   the overall rate is 69.2 %. The floor is not lowered: every source is listed with its reasons, and the entries left without
   a page are under §9, *Page not recovered*, with the sidecar's reason for each.
2. **What the recovery had to learn about these volumes.** AAS 1-12 (1909-1920) title the table of runs *Index generalis*
   *rerum*, not *actorum* (AAS 1 p. 833), and print, per category, one page per fascicle -- the page the section opens on, not
   the page of each act (AAS 4 (1912) 745: `EPISTOLAE, 23, 51, 98, 138 …`, where *Est sane* opens at 140) -- so a singleton run
   is read as the section up to the next section start of any category. AAS 2-7 (1910-1915) end the pope's part at
   `SACRAE CONGREGATIONES.` (AAS 2 (1910) 979) with no `ACTA` token; AAS 16 (1924) 507 opens `I. - ACTA £'11 PP. XI` and
   AAS 17 (1925) 671 splits the part end `IL - ACTA` / `SACRARUM CONGREGATIONUM` over two lines. Of 98 pages first refused for
   a running header that did not print the page number, 74 print no number at all (`Acta Pii PP. X.`) and 7 the number
   with one character wrong (`34i` for 344): both are accepted now, and the 14 still refused (§1b, *header*) are fascicle
   covers with a damaged `Num.` (`Num: 16`, `Nun. 13`, `Num. U`) and numbers two edits off. The last section start of a category
   runs to the pope part's end (the page before the *Index generalis*; AAS 7 (1915): `EPISTOLAE … 589` to 596, where three
   December letters open at pp. 591-593), a heading whose page list the OCR spoils (AAS 16 (1924) 507: `LITTERAE ENCYCLICAE, 5
   (12)`) constrains nothing instead of excluding every page, and a roman year set right after the month with no `anno`
   (`die x novembris MCMXV`, AAS 7 (1915) 569 -- the form Benedict XV's letters print) is read by the dating formula: the
   final review's regeneration moved 13 rows, three to *unique*, eight to *dated* and the two *Communis vestra* to *claimants*
   (the sidecars, 2026-09-21). Mapping the bare `ALLOCUTIO` the
   *Index generalis rerum* of AAS 6 and 8 prints made a bare `Allocutio` line inside AAS 12 (1920) 585-593's *Sacrum*
   *Consistorium* a heading of its own: eight consistory items are labelled *Allocutiones* there (five of them entries, §13,
   three without a page, §9), neither category harvested, no document moved -- an editorial point for the owner.
3. **The first sidecars accepted five pages that are not the acts', and the rule was tightened before the join ran.** A hit
   counted wherever the incipit's words opened a line or followed a full stop, which running text does too: *Parisiis*
   (27 September 1919) was given AAS 11 p. 109, where the word opens a wrapped line of *Dilectus filius* (the act opens at
   p. 457); *Promulgandi* (29 September 1908) AAS 1 p. 71, the *Ordo servandus* citing `Constit. Promulgandi` (the constitution
   opens at p. 5); *Solertiae* (12 September 1910) AAS 2 p. 562, a Secretariat of State letter that reads `constanter.
   Sollertiae vestrae`; the June 1921 *Consistorium publicum* the March consistory's page; and *Ex hac* (16 April 1924) AAS 16
   p. 270 by the dated rule, the formula it read on p. 269 being the previous act's, printed above the opening. Two of the five
   had been written into the data by the first run (*Promulgandi*, *Solertiae*). A paragraph head is now the salutation's dash
   or a line start under a heading, a numeral, a salutation or the memorial formula -- not under running text -- and the
   formula that settles a tie is read from the hit's own line on (`recover.ts`, with the pages quoted): the sidecars
   regenerated then (740 rows to 759, before the final review's 13 moves above) lost six -- three of the five (*Promulgandi* and
   *Ex hac* move to their true pages instead) and three consistories that open mid-sentence (*Consistorium secretum, cuius acta*)
   -- and gained 25 openings the false hits had made *several*. Every page accepted then was
   read against its context: 646 follow the salutation's dash, 113 open a line under a heading, a numeral or a salutation.
4. **What the OCR loses beyond the page: a year at the head of a run, which the dittos inherit.** AAS 9-I (1917) reads the
   `1916` over nine apostolic letters at pp. 57-69 as `1910` (the sample report §1), so the creator held all nine as dated before
   Benedict XV's election; AAS 3 (1911) reads the `1911` over the July letters as `191Í` (PDF p. 682), so ten letters of Pius X
   from July to November print no year at all. Nineteen rows of `ACTA_INDEX_CORRECTIONS` supply the year from each act's own
   dating formula, read in the store text at the page the recovery gave it: eight of the nine and nine of the ten are created
   (§8; *Romanorum Pontificum* of 25 February 1916 is held by the class guard beside `mag:benedict-xv/romanorum-pontificum-1916`,
   which the shelf files as a brief), and four
   dittos took a day the index does not print or prints wrongly -- *Laetamur utrumque* (12 July under a ditto of 10),
   *Societatem Goerresianam* (22 July), *Vobis plane* (`30` under July; the act is dated `xxx Sextilis`, 30 August), *Basilica*
   *B. M. V.* (20 May 1916 under a ditto of 13). Two acts the tables cannot reach because their key is the page and the page
   is another act's: *Communes litterae* (AAS 2 (1910) 905, dated `xxvi Octobris` at p. 906 where the index dittoes 13 October
   under *Binas nuper*, which opens the same page) and *Ubi accepimus* (AAS 3 (1911) 565, September with no day, the page
   *Societatem Goerresianam* opens). Both are left as the index prints them -- the first pair held (`page-shared`), the second
   held for its year -- and are the era's evidence that a curation key below the page is the mechanism the tables lack.
5. **Twelve pages print two acts; one prints one act and the index's page for the other is the OCR's.** Seven pages two
   created letters cite, both through the recovery (AAS 4 (1912) 269, AAS 5 (1913) 424, AAS 9-I (1917) 53 and 424, AAS 14
   (1922) 499, AAS 15 (1923) 510) or one through the index, and six pages two *matched* shelf letters cite (AAS 2 (1910) 51, 53,
   191, 446, 509; AAS 15 (1923) 353), were each read in the store text and print two short letters under one running header:
   curated in `ACTA_SHARED_PAGES` with both headings and both index lines quoted, twenty-four acts created or cited at their
   page. AAS 5 (1913) 361 opens *Eximiae caritatis* alone; the French letter to Wilhelm II (*L'échéance jubilaire*, 5 June 1913)
   the index cites at `361` opens at 362 (§9, *page-shared*, the true page recorded here for Task 9's readings).
6. **Every pope heading maps, and nine headings are new to the table.** `I. — ACTA PII PP. X.` heads 1909-1914 (the OCR's
   `IL` and `L` for the numeral in 1914-1925), Benedict XV's part opens in AAS 6 (1914) beside Pius X's and Pius XI's in AAS 14
   (1922) beside Benedict XV's. The singular `CONSTITUTIO APOSTOLICA` (AAS 5 (1913) *In praecipuis*, AAS 16 (1924) *Dominici*
   *gregis cura*; AAS 14 (1922) with the numeral read `T.`), the O spellings `EPISTOLAE ENCYCLICAE` (AAS 11 (1919) *In hac tanta*)
   and `EPISTOLA ENCYCLICA` (AAS 16 (1924) *Maximam gravissimamque*), the two-line `ADHORTATIO AD POPULORUM BELLIGERANTIUM
   MODERATORES` (AAS 9-I (1917) *Dès le début*, on the exhortations shelf), `LITTERAE AUTOGRAPHAE` (AAS 14 (1922) 703, two Italian
   letters, the chirographs' row), `PRECATIONUM FORMULAE` (AAS 13 (1921) 571, two prayers, *Orationes*) and, from the *Index*
   *generalis rerum* alone, `CONSTITUTIONES`, `ALLOCUTIO`, `MOTU PROPRJO`, `ACTA SACRORUM CONSISTORIORUM` and `MONITUM` (§4). No
   heading is left unknown; the OCR's `ANNO MUNSE DIE` (AAS 14 (1922) 709) is the column header, read as such.

*Opened without a page* is the parser's count before recovery (§1); *Recovered* the sidecar's rows, by the rule that accepted
each page (the only hit in the category's runs; the hit whose dating formula gives the entry's date, `(n fuzzy)` of them among hits
within one OCR character; the only hit within one OCR character); *Not recovered* by its reason (no hit; several hits and no formula
to settle them; hits outside the category's runs; a running header that contradicts the page; a page another entry of the same
category and incipit was given or already holds; an entry the index describes without an incipit). The rate after recovery counts the
recovered entries among the entries and every entry opened without a page among the lines -- (entries + recovered) / (page lines +
opened without a page) -- and the 95 % floor applies to it; a source under it is explained in the reading. Every unrecovered entry
is listed under §9, *Page not recovered*.

*Claimed twice* counts the documents two entries both match (§5), as the 2015–2024 report does; a document claimed from
two sources counts in each. *Unmatched* counts the entries of a harvested or partly harvested category the join left without a document (each is
listed in §6 with a belief); *Created* and *Held* partition them, with the ambiguous and doubly-claimed entries, by the
creator's rules (§8, §9). *Dated > 1 year before the volume* counts the acts a volume publishes late (spec §2: an entry can be
dated years earlier; 1917 prints letters of 1910 and 1915). *Documents of the popes without an entry* counts the harvested
shelf documents of the source's popes dated in the volume year that carry no `acta` (§11).

### The reading

1. **113 documents carry a reference into the seventeen volumes: 111 matched, every one from a quoted index line (§12) --
   21 incipit, 14 incipit-month, 76 unique -- and 2 curated (`ACTA_CURATED_REFERENCES`, below).** The shelves of Pius X and
   Benedict XV are thin -- vatican.va holds 306 records of Pius X and 63 of Benedict XV (§10) -- and
   Benedict XV's letters shelf is not harvested (279 *Epistulae* of 1912-1922 held for it, §9), so the era is a harvest more than
   a join. What matches cites its page: Pius X's *Communium rerum* (AAS 1 (1909) 333, recovered), *Iamdudum* (AAS 3 (1911) 217),
   *Lacrimabili statu* (AAS 4 (1912) 521, recovered) and *Singulari quadam* (AAS 4 (1912) 8); Benedict XV's *Humani generis*
   *redemptionem* (AAS 9-I (1917) 305), *Paterno iam diu* (AAS 11 (1919) 437, by incipit within the month), *Pacem, Dei munus*
   *pulcherrimum* (AAS 12 (1920) 209), *Spiritus Paraclitus* (AAS 12 (1920) 385), *Sacra propediem* (AAS 13 (1921) 33), *In*
   *praeclara summorum* (AAS 13 (1921) 209) and *Fausto appetente die* (AAS 13 (1921) 329), all recovered; Pius XI's *Rerum*
   *omnium perturbationem* (AAS 15 (1923) 49) and *Ecclesiam Dei* (AAS 15 (1923) 573), recovered, *Maximam gravissimamque* (AAS 16
   (1924) 5) and *Quas primas* (AAS 17 (1925) 593). Seven more are read by hand (`ACTA_PAGE_READINGS`, Task 9, every row
   quoting the page the act opens on and its dating formula, with an `ACTA_INDEX_CORRECTIONS` row keyed by that page where the
   index misdates the act or prints the month alone): Pius X's *Sapienti Consilio* (AAS 1 (1909) 7, dated 29 June 1908 where the
   index prints `Ian. 29`), Benedict XV's *Ad beatissimi Apostolorum Principis* (AAS 6 (1914) 565), *Quod iam diu* (AAS 10 (1918)
   473, the entry the index describes without an incipit), *Principi Apostolorum Petro* (AAS 12 (1920) 457) and *Annus iam*
   *plenus* (AAS 12 (1920) 553), Pius XI's *Post datam* (AAS 15 (1923) 193, the OCR's `i apr.` read as its incipit and August as
   its month) and *Latinarum litterarum* (AAS 16 (1924) 417). One reference no index entry can give is curated
   (`ACTA_CURATED_REFERENCES`): *Providentissima Mater Ecclesia* (27 May 1917) opens AAS 9 (1917) part II at p. 5, the volume of the
   Code, which has no chronological index; *Sacrae disciplinae leges* (AAS 75 (1983) part II, pp. VII-XIV, Roman-numbered)
   stays without one, `acta.page` being an integer. The second curated reference displaces a match (controller ruling 15,
   `supersedes`; §5): *Ubi arcano Dei consilio* (23 December 1922) had matched the 1923 index's *Fin dal primo momento* at AAS 15
   (1923) 5, the Italian printing of the January fascicle, because the 1922 index's line for the Latin (AAS 14 (1922) 673, `Ubi
   arcano Dei consilio. - Ad venerabiles fratres`) prints no date and opens no entry (§3) -- a reading is keyed to an entry the
   parser opens without a page, so none could name it, and a hold stops a creation, not a match. The row cites the Latin, which
   opens AAS 14 (1922) 673 under `LITTERAE ENCYCLICAE … DE PACE CHRISTI IN REGNO CHRISTI QUAERENDA`, dated at p. 700 `die xxiii
   Decembris MDCCCCXXII` (the store text, read 2026-09-21), and quotes the Italian page it displaces (`LETTERA-ENCICLICA … Fin dal
   primo momento`); the 1923 entry is neither a claim nor a record, as the vernaculars of 1929, 1933 and 1937 are held.
2. **458 documents created** (§8) -- `rp:benedict-xv` 112, `rp:pius-x` 159, `rp:pius-xi` 187 -- and 537 entries held (§9), 39 of them by the
   duplicate guard and 4 by the OCR rule, 6 by the page rule. The creations are the era: 192 letters of Pius X and Pius XI (whose letters shelves
   are harvested; Benedict XV's are held), 190 apostolic letters, 56 constitutions, 12 motu proprio, 4 *sub plumbo* letters and
   4 decretal letters -- eleven of them at a page read by hand (Task 9, §8 `page read`): *Tribus abhinc annis* (AAS 10 (1918)
   305, 1 July 1918 by its formula), the constitutions *Coenobium Sublacense* (AAS 7 (1915) 197) and *Archidioecesis*
   *Olindensis-Recifensis* (AAS 13 (1921) 463, 2 August 1918), *Praedecessorum nostrorum* (AAS 13 (1921) 252), *Eximia Benedictini*
   *Ordinis* (AAS 13 (1921) 290), the two *Apostolica Sedes* (AAS 15 (1923) 141 and 258; the first dated 16 July 1922 by its
   formula under the index's ditto of 1923, with *Romani Pontifices* for the same town at p. 137, 15 July 1922, which the
   correction frees from the same-incipit guard), *Ad munus pastorale* (AAS 16 (1924) 424) and *Vertit in animarum* (AAS 17
   (1925) 569); *Poiché ogni ragione* and *Bibliorum scientiam* (AAS 16 (1924) 177 and 180) come back by the recovery's own
   rule once the parser reads the 1924 index's `IV.?- MOTU PROPRIO` heading (the OCR's `?`, the one such heading of the
   fixtures). One entry prints toponym and incipit both; the provisional ids are 7: two of 1909, where the
   index describes the act and prints no incipit in guillemets, one of 1920 whose toponym the OCR broke (`J Guineae Gallicae`),
   two of 1916 whose opening words the parser does not read as an incipit for the abbreviation in them (*Rector Ecclesiae*
   *B. M. V.*, *Basilica B. M. V.*), and the two constitutions read by hand whose opening words the parser reads as a toponym
   (*Coenobium Sublacense*, *Archidioecesis Olindensis-Recifensis*). 488 are held by the rules that precede the guard: 279 for Benedict XV's letters
   shelf, 193 dated to the month, 4 ambiguous and 5 claimed twice (§5: *Delectarunt* and *Studium quo tenemur* of 3 May 1910
   against two shelf letters of the day under other incipits; three letters of 14 May 1923 against `perlibenti-sane-1923`,
   whose incipit the index prints as *Perlibenti* alone) and 2 *Epistulae Apostolicae* not created from the *Acta*; the OCR rule
   holds 4 whose incipit it damaged (`[an. 5 Dilecti filii`, `ACTA, vol`, `N ee excedere`, `ACT* , vol`), each for a curated reading,
   and 5 curated rows (`ACTA_HOLDS`) hold the entries whose incipit the OCR misspells into a well-formed word the body
   contradicts -- the fuzzy rule found their pages, and the body line the sidecar quotes is the evidence: *Una cum ofíiciosis*
   (AAS 5 (1913) 30: *officiosis*), *Incumbentes Nobis* (AAS 13 (1921) 186: *Incumbentis*), *Placet oculog* (AAS 13 (1921) 194:
   *oculos*), *Eapallensi in civitate* (AAS 17 (1925) 301: *Rapallensi*), *Pretioso purpúrala* (AAS 17 (1925) 302: *purpurata*) --
   as *Begnum Dei* (1959) is held. Two AAS-only ids move: `mag:pius-xi/supremi-apostolatus-1925` (AAS 18 (1926)
   86, 14 July 1925) becomes `supremi-apostolatus-1925-07-14` beside AAS 17 (1925)'s *Supremi apostolatus* of 12 May 1925, and
   `mag:benedict-xv/constat-apprime-1921` (AAS 13 (1921) 298, 16 April 1921) becomes `constat-apprime-1921-04-16` beside the two
   *Constat apprime* of 25 January and 20 October 1921 (pp. 191 and 493) the final review's formula reading settles, the
   collision pass giving each the full-date form; no shelf id is re-minted (§8).
3. **Dated years before the volume** (§7): the nineteen are AAS 9-I's -- the letters of 1915 the 1917
   volume prints late, and the nine of 1916 the OCR dated 1910 (§1.4) -- with *Sollicitis Nobis* (December 1910 in AAS 4) and
   the two constitutions of 1917 and 1918 (*Quod catholicae religionis*, *A multis*) AAS 13 (1921) prints at pp. 457 and 461, both
   created under their own dates.
4. **The acts of weight the recovery left without a page, and what Task 9's readings did with them** (§9, *Page not*
   *recovered*): every encyclical and constitution the block listed was read in the store text at a definite page and has a row
   -- Benedict XV's *Ad beatissimi Apostolorum Principis* (the recovery's *several*: p. 585 heads the encyclical's *versiones*
   *authenticae*), *Principi Apostolorum Petro* (*none*: the index's `Apostolomtm`), *Annus iam plenus* and the December 1918
   letter (*Quod iam diu*), the constitutions of 1915, 1918 and 1921-1925 and *Sapienti Consilio* (the drop capital) -- and the
   shelf records they cite are no longer in §11. Of the motu proprio, *Tribus abhinc annis* (1918) and the two shelf records
   awaiting a reference (*Post datam*, *Latinarum litterarum*) were read; the 1909 motu proprio the index describes without an
   incipit, *Vix dum* (1914) and *Quandoquidem* (1915) were not, none being a shelf record awaiting its reference, and the seven
   *sub plumbo* letters stay as the recovery left them. One reading opens a page the index also gives another act: *Inter*
   *praecipuas* (6 January 1925) opens AAS 17 (1925) 289, and the 1925 index's OCR sets that `289` on the line of *Ex Apostolico*
   *officio* (27 March 1925), which opens at p. 516 (`VALENTINA IN BRASILIA / ERECTIONIS DIOECESIS`, dated at p. 519); invariant 25
   now holds both (§9, *page-shared*), and the record that cited 289 for the second (`ex-apostolico-officio-1925`) is no longer
   created -- held, as *Casti connubii* (1930) is, until a page correction can be curated. The two holds the page-keyed tables
   cannot reach (§1.4) were read again and stay: *Communes litterae* is dated `die xxvi Octobris MDMX` at AAS 2 (1910) 906 and
   *Ubi accepimus* `die xxvi mensis Septembris MCMXI` at AAS 3 (1911) 566, but a correction is keyed by the page and gated by the
   printed date, and each page's key is another act's (`1910:905` would re-date *Binas nuper* too; `1911:565` is *Societatem*
   *Goerresianam*'s row).
5. **Documents of the era's popes dated in the volume years without a reference** (§11): 113, of which the
   formal genres are mostly the acts the index dates to the month (matched only by incipit, which the shelf's headings do not
   always print), the December acts (the next volume's), and Pius X's motu proprio of 1910 -- *Sacrorum antistitum*, *Cum*
   *per apostolicas*, *Illibatae custodiendae* -- whose months the 1910 index enters only month-only entries under; *Editae*
   *saepe* (26 May 1910) is in the 1910 index with no date on its line (`Editae saepe. - Litt. Encycl, expleto anno
   tercentesimo`, a line outside any entry, §3), as *Ubi arcano Dei consilio* is in 1922's.

## 4. The mappings decided

### Category headings

Every heading the era's sources print, as `normaliseHeading` renders it, with the row of `categories.ts` it maps to, the
registry class, the harvestedness and whether the creator creates from it. A heading printed in an OCR spelling is listed as
the fixture prints it. No heading was left `unknown` (§1).

| Heading (as printed) | Sources | Category row | Registry class | Harvested | Created from the Acta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ACTA SACRI CONSISTORII | 1917-I | Consistoria | — | no | no (not harvested) |
| ADHORTATIO AD POPULORUM BELLIOERANTIUM MODERATORES | 1917-I | Adhortationes Apostolicae | `apostolic-exhortation` | yes | yes (apost_exhortations shelf, per pope) |
| ALLOCUTIO | 1916, 1920 | Allocutiones | `discourse-address` | no | no (not harvested) |
| ALLOCUTIONES | 1911, 1912, 1913, 1914, 1915 | Allocutiones | `discourse-address` | no | no (not harvested) |
| APOSTOLICAE SUB PLUMBO LITTERAE | 1912, 1913, 1916, 1918 | Litterae Apostolicae sub plumbo datae | `papal-bull` (not apostolic-constitution) | partly | yes (bulls shelf, per pope) |
| CONSTITUTIO APOSTOLICA | 1913, 1918, 1922, 1924 | Constitutiones Apostolicae | `papal-bull`+apostolic-constitution | yes | yes (apost_constitutions/apost-constitutions shelf, per pope) |
| CONSTITUTIONES APOSTOLICAE | 1909, 1911, 1912, 1915, 1919, 1920, 1921, 1923, 1925 | Constitutiones Apostolicae | `papal-bull`+apostolic-constitution | yes | yes (apost_constitutions/apost-constitutions shelf, per pope) |
| DECRETA | 1915 | Decreta | — | no | no (not harvested) |
| DECRETUM | 1916 | Decreta | — | no | no (not harvested) |
| EPISTOLA APOSTOLICA | 1919, 1923, 1924 | Epistulae Apostolicae | `apostolic-letter` (not motu-proprio) | yes | no: the twelve unmatched of 2015-2024 are on the year-partitioned letters shelf, not harvested for Francis (#4); the two of Pius XII wait with them |
| EPISTOLA ENCYCLICA | 1924 | Litterae Encyclicae | `encyclical` | yes | yes (encyclicals shelf, per pope) |
| EPISTOLAE ENCYCLICAE | 1919 | Litterae Encyclicae | `encyclical` | yes | yes (encyclicals shelf, per pope) |
| EPISTOLAE | 1910, 1911, 1912, 1913, 1914, 1915, 1916, 1917-I, 1918, 1919, 1920, 1921, 1922, 1923, 1924, 1925 | Epistulae | `letter` | partly | yes (letters shelf, per pope) |
| HORTATIO | 1915 | Hortationes | `apostolic-exhortation` | partly | no: the heading covers the Lenten addresses to the parish priests of Rome (speeches, not harvested) beside one apostolic exhortation, which matched the shelf |
| LITTERAE APOSTOLICAE | 1909, 1910, 1911, 1912, 1913, 1915, 1916, 1917-I, 1918, 1919, 1920, 1921, 1922, 1923, 1924, 1925 | Litterae Apostolicae | `apostolic-letter` (not motu-proprio) | yes | yes (apost_letters shelf, per pope) |
| LITTERAE DECRETALES | 1920, 1925 | Litterae Decretales | `papal-bull` (not apostolic-constitution) | partly | yes (bulls shelf, per pope) |
| LITTERAE ENCYCLICAE | 1909, 1911, 1912, 1914, 1917-I, 1918, 1920, 1921, 1923, 1925 | Litterae Encyclicae | `encyclical` | yes | yes (encyclicals shelf, per pope) |
| MOTU PROPRIO | 1911, 1912, 1913, 1914, 1915, 1916, 1917-I, 1918, 1920, 1923, 1924, 1925 | Litterae Apostolicae Motu proprio datae | `apostolic-letter`+motu-proprio | yes | yes (motu_proprio shelf, per pope) |
| PRECATIONUM FORMULAE | 1921 | Orationes | — | no | no (not harvested) |
| SACRA CONSISTORIA | 1923 | Consistoria | — | no | no (not harvested) |
| SERMO | 1916, 1917-I, 1918, 1921 | Sermones | `discourse-address` | no | no (not harvested) |

Decisions taken here with the evidence beside each row of `categories.ts` (the earlier decisions stand): the singular
`CONSTITUTIO APOSTOLICA` (AAS 5, 1913; AAS 14, 1922, its numeral read `T.`; AAS 16, 1924) to the constitutions row, as the
volumes head a year with one; `EPISTOLAE ENCYCLICAE` (AAS 11, 1919) and `EPISTOLA ENCYCLICA` (AAS 16, 1924) to the encyclicals
row, the O spelling of the U forms 1933-1954 print; the two-line `ADHORTATIO AD POPULORUM BELLIGERANTIUM MODERATORES` (AAS 9-I,
1917, the OCR's `BELLIOERANTIUM`) to the exhortations row, since vatican.va files *Dès le début* on the apost_exhortations shelf;
`LITTERAE AUTOGRAPHAE` (AAS 14, 1922, p. 703: two Italian letters) to *Chirographa*; `PRECATIONUM FORMULAE` (AAS 13, 1921, p. 571:
two prayers) to *Orationes*, not harvested; and, from the *Index generalis rerum* of 1909-1920, which the recovery reads for the
page runs, `CONSTITUTIONES` (AAS 1-3), `ALLOCUTIO` (AAS 6, 8), `MOTU PROPRJO` (AAS 8, the OCR's `J`), `ACTA SACRORUM
CONSISTORIORUM` (AAS 11) and `MONITUM` (AAS 4, p. 695: the *Avvertenza* on the Italian newspapers not conforming to the letter
of 1 July 1911 to the Lombard bishops, no class of the registry's) to their rows. No heading is left `unknown`; `ANNO MUNSE DIE` (AAS 14, 1922, p. 709) is the column header, read
as `index.ts` reads `DXE` and `D1E`.

(1939, 1958) are not pope parts and are skipped with the dicasteries. The nested table of contents of 1909 consumed as
sub-items is keyed on 13 capitalised division titles listed in `index.ts`.

### Pages two matched acts cite, withheld

None: every page two matched documents of the era cite is curated in `ACTA_SHARED_PAGES` with the volume page quoted, and
invariant 25 admits exactly those pairs (10 pages of the era).

### Acts the Acta print twice

None among the era's sources.

### Matches a curated reference displaces

A row of `ACTA_CURATED_REFERENCES` (curation.ts) that names the match it supersedes, with the evidence that the matched entry is
not the act's citation of record (controller ruling 15): the match is neither a claim nor a record, and the document cites the row's page.
Entries of the era so superseded: AAS 15 (1923) 5 (*Fin dal primo momento*, `mag:pius-xi/ubi-arcano-dei-consilio-1922`; the citation of record is AAS 14 (1922) 673 by ACTA_CURATED_REFERENCES).

## 8. Created from the Acta

The data carries **458** AAS-only records from the era and the creator, re-run here over the shelf records, produces **458** — the same set, entry for entry, and the same records field for field (ids aside, which the collision and ordinal passes assign).

Each record carries `source.url` = the whole-volume PDF for a volume source and `null` for an index PDF (spec 2a §4), the
fixture's `retrieved` date, `source.shelf` `aas/{year}`, and `acta.part` for a double volume (`"I"` for 1917).

By class: apostolic-letter+motu-proprio 12; apostolic-letter 190; letter 192; papal-bull+apostolic-constitution 56; papal-bull 8. Provisional (no incipit read): 7.

**Circumscription material.** Of the 56 AAS-born constitutions of the era, the index describes 1 with an erection or elevation
verb (*conditur*, *erigitur*, *constituitur*, *dismembrato*, *evehitur*, *attollitur*, *extollitur*); none carries `keywords` or `actKind` (spec 2a §4).

### Shelf ids re-minted by densification

**None.** No AAS-only provisional record shares an ordinal group (issuer, genre, date) with a shelf provisional record, and
the shelf's ordinals are what a pass over the shelf records alone assigns; no minted shelf id gained a full-date suffix.

The era prints 0 radio messages: Vatican Radio was inaugurated on 12 February 1931 (*Qui arcano Dei*, AAS 23 (1931) 65, the
sample's), and the volumes of 1909–1925 precede it by six years and more. The count #27 asks for (`medium: radio`) is zero
here, and [#38](https://github.com/CatholicOS/cmddr/issues/38) carries the later eras' count.
